Changelog
=========

Every point release this port has made, newest first, each pinned to the
commit that delivered it and to the gate that verified it. A row is added
when a deliverable is verified, not when work starts.

Versions are milestones, not calendar releases. The first two numbers say
how far along the ladder in `doc/README.roadmap.md` the driver is; the
third is the point release and increments inside the current milestone.
Before 1.0 the number said nothing about stability. 1.0.0 is the first
tagged release, `v1.0.0`, and the number above it names a milestone the
tree has met rather than one it is working toward.

The numbers before 0.1.11 are assigned retroactively to the state at each
commit. Two rewrites of this repository's history have happened since,
so a hash here is the current one: `doc/history/` maps the first, and
the 0.2.7 row records the second.

`script/test-history.sh` reads this file: every row must pin a hash that
resolves, no two rows may share a version, and the subject of the commit
is printed beside the row so a wrong pin is visible rather than merely
resolvable. It also names any commit touching `src/` or `script/` that
has no row yet, which is a prompt and not a failure.

| 1.0.58 | 2026-10-27 | A reply frame names its call and its place, checked at receipt. The request now carries a per-connection tag that every feed echoes with its index, the close carries the count sent, the callback clears the state's call at the close, and a frame with no live call, another call's tag, an index out of order or a short count ends the link with its own line; `HAMMER2_NOCLIENTSEQ` compiles out the last three as the negative control. `cluster-wire.sh` gains `H2_WIRE_CUT=loss`, `dup`, `reorder`, `trail` and `late`, `H2_WIRE_SEQ=control` and `H2_REPEAT=n` | `35e3ca2` | under the control, call 304 of K 155 with 2 feeds on key 0000000000010000: `loss` verified with B one data line short of A on `h2kasan-7.3` (`wire-run-20261026-233247`) and `h2debug-7.3` (`-234533`); `dup` refused once as not a proxy with the link up on both (`-234021`, `-234751`); `late` verified with B holding the old call's block as the new call's on `h2debug-7.3` (`wire-run-20261027-000623`, at `807c0f3`); before the fix, `trail` ten times on `h2kasan-7.3` at `6296671` printed no KASAN report, so the use after free stays a `candidate`; `doc/history/verification-record.md`, the first 2026-10-27 entry |

| 1.0.37 | 2026-10-17 | Ten of the quick group's failures reread one at a time, five of them the port's, each read by the 2026-10-08 entry as the format's or as a surface not carried, and five defects fixed. A device node, fifo or socket takes the kernel's own file operations from `init_special_inode()` with the device number the create stored, where every type but a directory and a symlink had opened as an empty regular file. `->fallocate` takes `i_rwsem`, which `vfs_fallocate()` does not take for it, so an extending allocate no longer cuts off a write that passed its end. | 1.0.21 | 2026-10-13 | The cluster converged. The defect was a split that could not relieve its parent, and the fix bounds the split, not the radix. `hammer2_chain_create_indirect()` chooses the new block's size from the parent's TYPE: HAMMER2_IND_BYTES_MIN (4 KiB, 32 slots) under a directory inode, HAMMER2_IND_BYTES_NOM (16 KiB, 128 slots) under anything else. The keybits helper then raises the new block's radix until it holds `ncount`, so ncount 128 forces keybits 7. A parent at `0x480/7` is therefore asked for a child of its own width, and a keybits-7 child spans exactly a keybits-7 parent's keyspace: the split moves elements into a block covering the same range, the parent stays full, and the next insert splits again. Measured in the hang log: 4188 attempts at each of keys `0x460` through `0x4ec` into that one parent, 547925 inserts in a pass, until the run wedged. `ncount` is now bounded below `2^(parent keybits - 1)`, which lands the new block one radix level below its parent at every level; `pmax` is clamped to 128 because ncount never exceeds it, and the shift is on `hammer2_key_t` because a parent at keybits 62 exists here and `1U << 61` is undefined. Effect: `create_indirect` 536026 to 0, refused blocks 340 to 0, inserts 547925 to 11675. The slave's cluster PFS now holds inode 4801, dirent 4801 and data 4800 lines against the master's identical three, and `sync-empty-base.sh` reports 18 checks 0 failed. Clearing that pointer before the drop is what the same function already does by hand for `hmp->vchain.pmp` and `hmp->fchain.pmp`, and a NULL `pmp` is a state the chain code expects and tests for everywhere it reads one.
